" data-portal-copyright= " Malcolm Parry " **28;57004;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1164687 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; ABOUT TIME : Another fourth-decade landmark restaurant , Bridges , is due for a $15-million renovation . So said George Frankel , who built the Granville Island waterfront facility and , with son Daniel , bought out his surviving partners in 2018 . A building permit arrived this week . Beginning in fall 2020 , work will involve wraparound terraces on both floors . Daniel , who runs the family firm today , also owns all three Tap &amp;amp ; Barrel pub-restaurants and Brewhall , the former Steel Toad Brewery . At the Omega boutique&amp;rsquo ; s recent annual reception , Frankel pere greeted Matteo Escoto , 9 , who appeared in past columns modelling the Swiss firm&amp;rsquo ; s wristwatches . Frankel wore a competing Rolex Oyster that was a gift from Daniel but vowed to reciprocate with a like-value Omega . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;img src= " https : **34;57034;TOOLONG ... title= " With 50 or so books produced to date and more on the way , expatriate logger-turned-author David Day will return to his native Victoria by summer . " alt= " With 50 or so books produced to date and more on the way , expatriate logger-turned-author David Day will return to his native Victoria by summer . " data-portal-copyright= " Malcolm Parry " **28;57070;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1164685 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; DAY&amp;rsquo ; S AHEAD : Victoria-born former logger David Day has written and helped provide paper for more books than many have read : 50 and counting . His Tolkien Bestiary sold a million copies . His Doomsday Book of Animals , with a foreword by the Duke of Edinburgh , sold 750,000 . Pal Terry Jones , the Monty Python Flying Circus team member , wrote the introduction for his Decoding Wonderland . Three more in Day&amp;rsquo ; s Tolkien series were released recently in North America , the UK and France . With wife Roisin , the long-time expatriate will soon occupy a former Day-family home in Victoria . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;img src= " https : **34;57100;TOOLONG ... title= " Journalist-author Stevie Cameron 's brother , artist-drummer-singer Chris Dahl , has released the Silver Dagger single from his Smoke + Shadows album " alt= " Journalist-author Stevie Cameron 's brother , artist-drummer-singer Chris Dahl , has released the Silver Dagger single from his Smoke + Shadows album " data-portal-copyright= " Malcolm Parry " **28;57136;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1164684 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; HI-YO SILVER : Further up-Island , Qualicum Beach&amp;rsquo ; s Ceramics Artworks firm owner Chris Dahl has released the Silver Dagger title track of a self-produced world-fusion album titled Smoke + Shadows . Artist Dahl drummed with the My Indole Ring band that had its self-titled 1969 album re-issued 41 years later by a producer in then-psychedelia-crazy Germany . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;img src= " https : **34;57166;TOOLONG ... title= " Seen here with Vanishing Tattoo partner Thomas Lockhart and Chili Dog , Vince Hemingson exhibits non-tattooed nudes and other photos in Kitsilano. " alt= " Seen here with Vanishing Tattoo partner Thomas Lockhart and Chili Dog , Vince Hemingson exhibits non-tattooed nudes and other photos in Kitsilano. " data-portal-copyright= " Malcolm Parry " **28;57202;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1164683 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; AFTER TATS : Vince Hemingson pinpointed communities in Borneo , California , China , Hawaii , Japan , the Philippines , Russia , Samoa , and other locales for a documentary titled The Vanishing Tattoo ( vanishingtattoo.com ) . No cutaneous embroidery appears on the female subjects of his Nude In The Landscape photographs exhibited at 1725 West Third to Dec. 31 , along with others of Asian locales and African wildlife . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; DOWN PARRYSCOPE : All bluster aside , modern-day &amp;ldquo ; witch hunts&amp;rdquo ; may actually identify witchcraft. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;a href= " **27;57232;TOOLONG " **34;57261;TOOLONG &lt;br&gt; 604-929-8456 &lt;/p&gt; Malcolm ParryFri , 13 Dec 2019 20:20:26 **45;57297;TOOLONG ... &amp;amp ; FitnessLocal ArtsLocal BusinessLocal **36;57344;TOOLONG review : In Vancouver After Dark , Aaron Chapman documents the once wild nightlife of the cityhttp : **36;57382;TOOLONG ... &lt;img src= " https : **34;57420;TOOLONG ... title= " Aaron Chapman is an author and historian whose most recent title is Vancouver After Dark : The Wild History of a City 's Nightlife . " alt= " Aaron Chapman is an author and historian whose most recent title is Vancouver After Dark : The Wild History of a City 's Nightlife . " data-portal-copyright= " " **28;57456;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1159000 " /&gt; &lt;h3&gt;Vancouver After Dark&lt;/h3&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;strong&gt;The Wild History of a City 's Nightlife&lt;/strong&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; **26;57486;TOOLONG Aaron Chapman ( Arsenal Pulp Press ) **26;57514;TOOLONG &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; $32.95 246 pages &lt;/p&gt; &lt;hr&gt; &lt;p&gt; In January 18 , 1971 , a page 2 ad in The Province announced a new entertainment at Club Zanzibar at 1129 Howe Street . &amp;ldquo ; Topless Wraslin&amp;rsquo ; . A Canadian First . See It to Believe It . Topless dancers trained to kick , maul , pinch , eye-gouge and hair-pull . Zany &amp;mdash ; Wacky &amp;mdash ; Hilarious &amp;mdash ; Violent . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; **25;57542;TOOLONG &lt;img src= " https : **34;57569;TOOLONG ... title= " " alt= " " data-portal-copyright= " " **28;57605;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " " /&gt; Hot baron of beef buffet rounded out the package . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; There was public outcry over this entertainment , as well as the want ads in The Vancouver Sun seeking 3 midgets to appear in a different &amp;ldquo ; wraslin ' &amp;rdquo ; event at the Gulf Club billed as &amp;ldquo ; one mad dog vs. five chicks. &amp;rdquo ; This was a far cry from bloody ground and pound MMA matches broadcast at most pubs today . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; It paled in comparison to far more zany , hilarious and &amp;mdash ; yes &amp;mdash ; violent stuff happening all over Vancouver at the time . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; In Vancouver After Dark , local historian Aaron Chapman shines a spotlight on &amp;ldquo ; the wild history of a city&amp;rsquo ; s nightlife . &amp;rdquo ; It took decades for the No Fun City designation to take hold . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; This is the third title in what the author describes as his trilogy , beginning with the story of the Penthouse in Liquor , Lust , and the Law ( 2012 ) and followed by Live at the Commodore ( 2014 ) . The Last Gang in Town ( 2017 ) , about the notorious Clark Park Gang , can be seen as a kind of &amp;ldquo ; adjunct title . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Responding to people&amp;rsquo ; s comments that Vancouver is too young to have any interesting history , Chapman frames his response academically : &amp;ldquo ; Crap . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; For a long time , I think that the history of Vancouver that was presented was usually another story about the railroad arriving , the three greenhorns and real estate , or the standard Chamber of Commerce creation story , &amp;rdquo ; said Chapman . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; But just scratch the surface a little and there is so much to learn , and it&amp;rsquo ; s gritty . That&amp;rsquo ; s has been something of a thread line through all of my books , I suppose . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;h2 class= " cat-head short " **40;57635;TOOLONG ... &lt;ul class= " relatedlinks " &gt; &lt;li&gt;&lt;a href= " http : **34;57677;TOOLONG ... about books and **28;57713;TOOLONG &lt;li&gt;&lt;a href= " http : **34;57743;TOOLONG ... latest Metro Vancouver arts **25;57779;TOOLONG &lt;/ul&gt; &lt;p&gt; As Chapman notes in his introduction , Vancouver today &amp;ldquo ; sold to the outside world depicts bright bustling neighbourhoods full of boutique shops , set against a backdrop of photogenic beaches and mountains , both of which can be visited in the same afternoon . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; As they do today , a century ago , pious city and provincial governments pursued teetotal agendas to put down opportunities to party . Persistent police raids , new noise legislation , and other machinery of the state couldn&amp;rsquo ; t keep the crowds from cramming into everything from bars and ballrooms to nightclubs and repurposed community halls . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Reading through Chapman&amp;rsquo ; s research , it&amp;rsquo ; s clear that access to entertainment spots in different neighbourhoods was far better in the past . But , he notes , there has always been a kind of entertainment district trend in town . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; The whole club scene has moved around so much over the years , from Hornby , to Gastown to Granville , and each time people decry the loss of venues , &amp;rdquo ; he said . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; Everyone I interviewed talked about how they saw the glory years of Vancouver , when it was the best time ever . But that changes with every generation , and there was a kid last night who went to the Commodore and saw a show which they will reflect back upon 20 years from now as the best time in the city . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; While there is no question that the scene has changed , particularly when it comes to the amount of live music venues , a healthy and active club scene still exits . New spaces pop-up in surprising places , just as they did in the past , and Chapman acknowledges that . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; One thing that Vancouver After Dark makes abundantly clear is that places come and go with frequency . From such East Side cabarets as the New Delhi , where mixed-race rockers like the Hi-Fives filled the dance floor , to any number of comic legend Tommy Chong&amp;rsquo ; s clubs back in the Sixties , tear it down and sanitize has always been a dominant development theme in the Lower Mainland . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;img src= " https : **34;57806;TOOLONG ... title= " The Hi-Fives perform at the New Delhi cabaret in Vancouver 's East End . " alt= " The Hi-Fives perform at the New Delhi cabaret in Vancouver 's East End . " data-portal-copyright= " " **28;57842;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1158894 " /&gt; &lt;h2 class= " cat-head short " **40;57872;TOOLONG ... &lt;ul class= " relatedlinks " &gt; &lt;li&gt;&lt;a href= " http : **34;57914;TOOLONG ... about books and **28;57950;TOOLONG &lt;li&gt;&lt;a href= " http : **34;57980;TOOLONG ... latest Metro Vancouver arts **25;58016;TOOLONG &lt;/ul&gt; &lt;p&gt; With every subsequent new luxury condo tower put up , more rules arrived . Add in skyrocketing licensing fees , rents , new noise bylaws , and more and it becomes hard to believe that anyone would choose to operate a business catering to nightlife . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; But even so , there have always been people in Vancouver who have been managing clubs or putting on a party somewhere and dealing with all of the levels of red tape and challenge , &amp;rdquo ; said Chapman . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; In as much as I do try to be positive about the future , the one thing that can&amp;rsquo ; t be ignored is that guys like Danny Beceda and Frank Hook essentially opened Oil Can Harry&amp;rsquo ; s before they were 25 . I don&amp;rsquo ; t see anyone that age being able to do that now with costs what they are , and maybe if that can be fixed we will find ourselves in another golden era. &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; What most certainly has transpired with the homogenization of culture in the city is that people are far less likely to have regular hangouts. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Chapman fondly recalls how the front lounge at the Town Pump was a meeting point for the whole of the Vancouver music scene for years &amp;mdash ; a place where hard rockers rubbed elbows with rockabilly musicians , industrial growlers and pop stars , the &amp;lsquo ; Pump was a nexus for live music fans and players . The original Railway Club was another such hangout. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Such locations often had intergenerational memory . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; When these places go and disappear on us , a part of the DNA of the city changes a bit , but that is a theme that runs up and down the whole West Coast as cities have grown and gentrified. &amp;rdquo ; Chapman said . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; We were fortunate to have a situation dating back to the vaudeville days where tours would make it up here that never went over the mountains to Calgary or Edmonton . Major tours still kick off here or finish up here a lot of the time , and artists frequently will be here early to rehearse or stick around after the tour ends , which has added to the entertainment scene as well . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Vancouver After Dark delves into the way that the changing performance market paved the way for many of the older cabarets to shut down . As acts that used to grace their stages developed the ability to pack far larger venues , they no longer needed to do a few nights in a row to make the same amount of money that they could with one night . In the closing chapter , Last Call , Chapman notes how this reality has changed the Granville Strip , where more pubs have opened in place of live or even dance music venues . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Many of the buildings that once housed live music venues are still standing , potentially to be reopened . But the likelihood is that this won&amp;rsquo ; t happen . Chapman notes &amp;ldquo ; time and again , the threat to a Vancouver club hasn&amp;rsquo ; t been a fire or mismanagement but property development . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; But there is still &amp;ldquo ; room for graveyard shift dreamers . &amp;rdquo ; &amp;nbsp ; Just don&amp;rsquo ; t try to revive topless wrasslin&amp;rsquo ; . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;h2 class= " cat-head short " **40;58043;TOOLONG ... &lt;ul class= " relatedlinks " &gt; &lt;li&gt;&lt;a href= " http : **34;58085;TOOLONG ... about books and **28;58121;TOOLONG &lt;li&gt;&lt;a href= " http : **34;58151;TOOLONG ... latest Metro Vancouver arts **25;58187;TOOLONG &lt;/ul&gt; &lt;img src= " https : **34;58214;TOOLONG ... title= " The Cave nightclub staff in Feb. 1949. " alt= " The Cave nightclub staff in Feb. 1949. " data-portal-copyright= " " **28;58250;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1162004 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;em&gt;The following is an excerpt from Vancouver After Dark . Images and text reprinted with permission from the publisher . &lt;/em&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;h3&gt;The Cave&lt;/h3&gt; &lt;p&gt; If there is one name on the list of all the nightclubs that have come and gone over the decades in Vancouver whose loss is perhaps most lamented , it&amp;rsquo ; s the Cave . There are even those who never went there who regret its demise , longing for the magic and excitement of a lost age . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The Cave was the city&amp;rsquo ; s premier venue during the height of the nightclub show era &amp;mdash ; the early 1960s , when nightlife was stylish and sophisticated . Many Vancouverites even remember its kitschy faux-cavern interior of papier-m&amp;acirc ; ch&amp;eacute ; stalactites and stalagmites with fondness , wishing the club were still here . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Gordon King opened the Cave at 626 Hornby Street in 1938 . King had come to town from Winnipeg , where he&amp;rsquo ; d opened the first Cave nightclub in 1935 . He later opened another one in Edmonton . The Vancouver Cave lasted the longest , though , and was the most famous , attracting many big-name American acts that toured along the Pacific coast . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; In addition to its subterranean decor , the club featured a special sprung dance floor and a large forty-foot-wide stage . In the early years , the venue hosted performers from the vaudeville era and veteran stars who&amp;rsquo ; d made their names in radio and cinema . Local dancers and performers also appeared , along with comedian emcees and even the odd magician. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The Cave changed management and ownership frequently during its history . After World War II , Gordon King&amp;rsquo ; s son Max returned from service in the Royal Canadian Air Force to run the club , until Isy Walters , who bought it in 1952 , sold it in 1958 and then opened Isy&amp;rsquo ; s Supper Club a year later . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Before they bought the Cave from Walters , Ken Stauffer and Bob Mitten had both worked at the Arctic Club , one of several members-only cocktail and supper clubs that were popular in Vancouver in the late 1940s and &amp;rsquo ; 50s . The two had the sort of good gamblers&amp;rsquo ; instincts that are crucial for being an effective nightclub owner . For instance , they often took chances on booking unknown acts , and most of the time , their intuition was proved right . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Since vaudeville days , Vancouver had been known as Tune-Up City , where performers could rehearse a show in front of a live audience before taking the act down the Pacific coast , through Seattle , Portland , San Francisco , and on to Los Angeles . The same routes exist today in modern concert touring . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; As far as club gigs went for local musicians , there were few better regular jobs than the Cave . The orchestra positions that paid union scale were coveted ones that most musicians in Vancouver did their best to keep . However , a few recent histories of the Cave suggest there was a colour barrier that kept black musicians from becoming orchestra members there . Harlem Nocturne proprietor and trombonist Ernie King stated , &amp;ldquo ; I was qualified enough to play in The Cave , but they didn&amp;rsquo ; t want a guy like me , they wanted an all-white band , not a coloured band with me sitting there . There were never any black musicians , unless it was a black band from the States . &amp;rdquo ; 1 &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Veteran Vancouver saxophonist Gavin Walker agrees race was a factor for musicians seeking entry into some Vancouver nightclub orchestras at the time and notes that the Cave orchestra in particular presented other challenges . &amp;ldquo ; The guys that got the orchestra positions at the Cave&amp;mdash ; &amp;lsquo ; The Downtowners&amp;rsquo ; was their unofficial name&amp;mdash ; were very protective of their union scale work , and it was a bit of a closed circle . You had to have the ability to play but also be a top-notch sight-reader . The Downtowners also got a lot of CBC work , and when the jazz shows came up on the CBC , these were the guys the CBC hired , not the hard-core jazz players who were mostly white that played other venues like the Jazz Cellar . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; One musician who did make the cut was Blaine Tringham , who began playing at the Cave as a junior orchestra member in his early twenties . &amp;ldquo ; A freelance horn player could make a pretty good living just by answering the phone and saying yes , &amp;rdquo ; Tringham says with a laugh . &amp;ldquo ; At the Cave , initially you&amp;rsquo ; d be the third or fourth call , but eventually you&amp;rsquo ; d work your way up to the first if you were any good . But in those days , the protocol was that whoever called you first got the gig , and I&amp;rsquo ; d take any gig I could . So sometimes you&amp;rsquo ; d take a gig at some other place like Isy&amp;rsquo ; s , then the Cave might call you and you&amp;rsquo ; d have to tell them you were already booked , even if you preferred to play there . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Tringham recalls that pay at the Cave was $160 a week and $120 a week at Isy&amp;rsquo ; s , because the hours were shorter . &amp;ldquo ; That doesn&amp;rsquo ; t sound like a lot today of course , but you have to remember this was the early 1960s . The rent in my furnished apartment was $65 a month , so you could make a good living as a club musician then . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; And with the number of nightclub acts that came through town at the Cave in the 1960s , Tringham stayed busy , but the schedule left little turnaround time to rehearse new acts . In the daytime , there were three-hour rehearsals to go over two ninety-minute shows . The constant flow of stars required a regular team of skilled musicians with seasoned chops on the Cave bandstand . In suits and ties , heads down in the charts on their music stands , Fraser MacPherson&amp;rsquo ; s band appeared completely focused and reserved from an audience perspective , but Tringham confesses things were not always what they seemed . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; The guys at the Cave could drink , &amp;rdquo ; he says . &amp;ldquo ; They had a capacity you couldn&amp;rsquo ; t believe . I was just the kid and didn&amp;rsquo ; t drink at the shows because I was too nervous to screw up a part or miss a cue . But the regular Cave musicians bought Beefeater gin by the case and kept it backstage in these lockers that were supposed to be for clothes . They&amp;rsquo ; d always have a cup of it at their feet , sipping it , and gin just looked like water . Every night those guys got loaded . Sometimes they&amp;rsquo ; d send the busboy to the liquor store to get more . The amazing thing was they never screwed up . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Tringham continues , &amp;ldquo ; There was a guy named Stew Barnett&amp;mdash ; he was the king of the trumpet at the Cave . As many drinks as he sipped away at , he never made a mistake through the whole show . It was only at the very end of the night he&amp;rsquo ; d run out of gas . The curtain would close and he couldn&amp;rsquo ; t get out of the chair . It was like a marathon runner who&amp;rsquo ; d hit the finish line . He was a great guy and a great musician . Everybody smoked and drank there while they played , taking a quick puff of a cigarette between parts . It was like that in the recording studios , too . That&amp;rsquo ; s just what you did then ! &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The Cave orchestra had their favourite performers to work with . Mitzi Gaynor rehearsed her whole nightclub show in Vancouver before taking it to Las Vegas , and came back year after year . Tringham recalls that although Mel Torm&amp;eacute ; had a reputation for being difficult , the band enjoyed playing with him and he was a complete professional . Anthony Newley , meanwhile , &amp;ldquo ; confessed to us he was really nervous with this debut at the Cave because it was a new nightclub show he was trying out . But he was a lot of fun . Just one of the guys . &amp;rdquo ; Another favourite was singer and actress Jaye P. Morgan , who might be best remembered as a TV game show panellist during the 1970s. &amp;ldquo ; She was very outspoken about&amp;mdash ; how shall I put this ? &amp;mdash ; who she liked . There was a guy in the band she kept hitting on in front of us , saying that she was going to take him to bed . She was teasing him like crazy , even during the shows . It was very funny . One night his wife came down to see the show and he sweated through the whole night hoping Jaye wouldn&amp;rsquo ; t say anything . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Over the years , the Cave featured many touring nightclub acts : older acts like the Mills Brothers and Roy Orbison appeared along with more modern performers like Diana Ross and the Supremes , James Brown , the Righteous Brothers , Sonny &amp;amp ; Cher , and Bette Midler. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;img src= " https : **34;58280;TOOLONG ... title= " Cave gig poster . " alt= " Cave gig poster . " data-portal-copyright= " " **28;58316;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1162002 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; Danny Baceda , who operated Oil Can Harry&amp;rsquo ; s jazz club , bought the Cave for $100,000 in 1971 . A month later , he bought Isy&amp;rsquo ; s Supper Club , making him the biggest nightclub owner in Vancouver at the time . But the expansion happened too quickly , and by 1972 , Baceda went into receivership . Ken Stauffer bought back the Cave in 1973 , running it briefly before selling it to Stan Grozina. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Originally from Slovenia , Grozina spent his early years in a series of German labour camps during World War II . He immigrated to Canada with twenty dollars in his pocket and soon became involved in the construction business in Winnipeg . He retired in Vancouver and bought the Cave , sinking $75,000 into renovations . But when the Cave reopened , Grozina&amp;rsquo ; s bookings were hit and miss . The club had success with rock bands like the Animals , Canned Heat , Trooper , and Doug and the Slugs , and Grozina occasionally booked some exciting emerging underground bands as well , like the local new wave band Pointed Sticks in 1979 . The musicals he booked weren&amp;rsquo ; t as successful , however , and with disco taking off in some downtown clubs , and punk rock emerging in others , the Cave was suddenly a horribly old-fashioned place to go . At that point , Grozina began booking wet T-shirt contests&amp;mdash ; things were going downhill fast . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; They had , in fact , been heading that way for a while . The golden era of nightclub acts was over . In many ways , the clubs had become victims of the performers&amp;rsquo ; success . The acts the nightclubs had nurtured had now become too successful to stay there , and the top performers who had once booked ten-day or two-week club engagements now came for one- or two-night stops at the Queen Elizabeth Theatre or , if they were big enough , the Pacific Coliseum . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The Cave was unable to make the transition the Commodore Ballroom did . The Commodore had once seen an older generation of entertainment but had made the jump to a modern concert space , thanks to owner Drew Burns , whose policy of renting the room to outside promoters ultimately gave the club its future . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Stan Grozina was no Drew Burns . He mistakenly hoped the old nightclub-style acts would live to see another day , and he could not imagine the Cave as any other kind of establishment . A disastrous booking in 1978 of an aged Ginger Rogers resulted in a $40,000 loss that doomed the club financially . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; You could really feel that things were changing by the early 1970s , &amp;rdquo ; recalls Tringham . For him , and other musicians like him , the very music they were being called to play was changing . &amp;ldquo ; I feel lucky to have been at the Cave when I did . It was the right place at the right time , &amp;rdquo ; he says . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The Cave closed in 1981 , and aside from a few expressions of nostalgia , there seemed to be little public outcry that the iconic club would be no more . The Cave was demolished that summer . Some of the club&amp;rsquo ; s old stalactites and stalagmites were saved and auctioned off . The Hong Kong Bank of Canada Tower was built in its place , marking the end of an era when the Hornby Street strip of nightclubs thrived . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Hornby is a different street now in a different city . There are no enticing neon signs or doormen on the street ushering you into the pleasures of the nightclubs inside . Only a few lonely taxi cabs glide by , heading to and from more exciting destinations . There is nothing to indicate this short stretch was were the city came to be entertained at the Cave and other thriving clubs . The sights and sounds of Vancouver&amp;rsquo ; s nightlife have moved elsewhere . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;img src= " https : **34;58346;TOOLONG ... title= " July 25 , 1981 . Demolition of The Cave nightclub . Deborah Cameron photo . " alt= " July 25 , 1981 . Demolition of The Cave nightclub . Deborah Cameron photo . " alt= " Melissa Benoist who stars in the Vancouver-shot TV series Supergirl. Condolences to his family and loved ones x&lt;/a&gt;&lt;/p&gt; &lt;p style= " color : #c9c8cd ; font-family:Arial , sans-serif ; font-size:14px ; line-height:17px ; margin-bottom:0 ; margin-top:8px ; overflow:hidden ; padding:8px 0 7px ; text-align:center ; text-overflow:ellipsis ; white-space:nowrap ; " &gt;A post shared by &lt;a href= " https : **33;65372;TOOLONG ... style= " color : #c9c8cd ; font-family:Arial , sans-serif ; font-size:14px ; font-style:normal ; font-weight:normal ; line-height:17px ; " target= " blank " &gt; Henry Golding&lt;/a&gt; ( @henrygolding ) on &lt;time style= " font-family:Arial , sans-serif ; font-size:14px ; line-height:17px ; " datetime= " **25;65407;TOOLONG " &gt;Nov 27 , 2019 at 1:27am **45;65434;TOOLONG ... &lt;script async src= " **28;65481;TOOLONG " &gt;&lt;/sc ... &lt;p&gt; Actor Henry Golding , who starred in last year&amp;rsquo ; s Crazy Rich Asians and has been filming in Vancouver this fall , also posted a message for Gao to Instagram. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; Very sad to hear of the passing of @godfreygao , a huge loss to the community , &amp;rdquo ; wrote Golding. &amp;ldquo ; He inspired so many to follow in his footsteps in film and entertainment , myself included . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Gao was born in Taipei , in 1984 , to a Taiwanese father and a Malaysian mother but grew up in North Vancouver . He went to Queensbury elementary , Argyle secondary and studied at Capilano University before heading to Taipei in 2004 to start his acting career appearing in Taiwanese television dramas . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; I wanted to make a name first here ( in Asia ) before trying to go for Hollywood , &amp;rdquo ; Gao told the Vancouver Sun in 2013. &amp;ldquo ; Now I hope to get more work in North America , but I&amp;rsquo ; d like to keep playing ( the two markets ) off each other . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;img src= " https : **34;65511;TOOLONG ... title= " Actor Godfrey Gao died Tuesday . " alt= " Actor Godfrey Gao died Tuesday . " data-portal-copyright= " Ethan Miller " **28;65547;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1154732 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; In 2011 , he became the first Asian model to be signed by Louis Vuitton . He made his American film debut in 2013 , starring as Magnus Bane in The Mortal Instrument : City of Bones , alongside Lily Collins , Jamie Campbell Bower and Kevin Zegers. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Chinese official media Zhejiang News confirmed Gao&amp;rsquo ; s death and said his body would be transported back to Taipei on Wednesday . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;em&gt; -- with files from Postmedia&lt;/em&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;a href= " **27;65577;TOOLONG " target= " blank " rel= " noopener " **34;65606;TOOLONG &lt;br&gt; &lt;a href= " http : **26;65642;TOOLONG " target= " blank " rel= " noopener " **32;65670;TOOLONG ... &lt;/p&gt; &lt;iframe width= " 640 " height= " 360 " src= " https : **34;65704;TOOLONG ... frameborder= " 0 " allow= " accelerometer ; autoplay ; encrypted-media ; gyroscope ; picture-in-picture " **34;65740;TOOLONG &lt;iframe width= " 640 " height= " 360 " src= " https : **34;65776;TOOLONG ... frameborder= " 0 " allow= " accelerometer ; autoplay ; encrypted-media ; gyroscope ; picture-in-picture " **34;65812;TOOLONG &lt;p&gt; &amp;nbsp ; &lt;/p&gt; Scott BrownWed , 27 Nov 2019 21:19:04 **45;65848;TOOLONG ... NewsQ&amp;amp ; A : Vancouver comedian Graham Clark prepares for 24-hour stand-up sethttp : **37;65895;TOOLONG ... &lt;img src= " https : **34;65934;TOOLONG ... title= " Graham Clark . " alt= " Graham Clark . " data-portal-copyright= " Handout " **28;65970;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1151829 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; Vancouver comedian Graham Clark will be taking stand-up to the extreme next weekend when he attempts a marathon , &lt;a href= " https : **33;66000;TOOLONG ... set for charity&lt;/a&gt; . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Clark , a three-time Canadian Comedy Award winner and the co-host of the popular and long-running Stop Podcasting Yourself , will take the stage at East Vancouver&amp;rsquo ; s Havana Theatre on Friday , Nov. 29 at 8 p.m . He intends to leave the stage at 9 p.m &amp;mdash ; on Saturday . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Inspired by fellow comedian Pat Thornton of Toronto , who has completed five such marathon shows to raise funds for the Stephen Lewis Foundation , Graham will soon become the first Vancouver comic to attempt the same . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; We caught up with Clark to find out what possesses someone to do something like this , how he plans to pull it off , and if he&amp;rsquo ; s allowed bathroom breaks . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;img src= " https : **34;66035;TOOLONG ... title= " " alt= " " data-portal-copyright= " " **28;66071;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;strong&gt;Q . What possesses a man to do 24 hours of stand-up ? &lt;/strong&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; A. The Stephen Lewis foundation encourages people to do dares to raise money for their charity . Pat Thornton has been doing this for years in Toronto , and Alistair Cook , the wonderful human who runs the Havana Theatre , dared me to do the same . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;strong&gt;Your marathon is to raise funds for The Stephen Lewis Foundation , to fights HIV and AIDS in Africa . Why did you choose this charity ? &lt;/strong&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; It&amp;rsquo ; s a wonderful charity , and it&amp;rsquo ; s their dare campaign that lead to this , so it only makes sense that they should benefit from it . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;strong&gt;Do you actually plan to stay standing , or is this more likely to be 24 hours of sit-down comedy ? &lt;/strong&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; I&amp;rsquo ; m going to stand as much as possible . I may lean some , and sure , if the occasion arises , I may sit . But I will be wearing my most comfortable shoes . I should get Dr. Scholl&amp;rsquo ; s on board next time . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;strong&gt;Do you even have that much material ? &lt;/strong&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Not even close . Luckily , I have some of Vancouver&amp;rsquo ; s best and brightest comedians coming down to write material for me as it goes on . Hopefully people online will join in as well . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;strong&gt;The Havana is a pretty dimly-lit venue . Are you concerned at all about your audience , or your yourself , falling asleep , since that 's what normal people do when it gets late ? &lt;/strong&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; I am always concerned about this , not just at this show ! &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;strong&gt;Forget holding people 's attention . Let 's talk about holding your bladder . What 's the plan for bathroom breaks ? &lt;/strong&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; I&amp;rsquo ; m going to take the occasional bathroom break , but will be running an hour longer to compensate for those . As far as the plan for those bathroom breaks , that is privileged information . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;strong&gt;This has never been attempted in Vancouver . What makes you think you can pull of something no Vancouverite has ever done ? Do you think you 're special , Graham ? &lt;/strong&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; If you ask my mother , yes , I&amp;rsquo ; m special . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;strong&gt;You recently served as the west coast correspondent for This Hour Has 22 Minutes . Does n't that seem like a more reasonable amount of time to be consistently funny ? &lt;/strong&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Ha ! Actually that would be a great name for this event : This Hour Has 24 Hours . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;strong&gt;Did you ever consider just running an actual marathon instead ? It would only take you , like , four or five hours . You 'd be home for dinner . &lt;/strong&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; It might only take you five hours . I think this will be a much shorter time span than it would take me to finish an actual marathon . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;strong&gt;Finally , are there any other local comics you 'd like to see attempt a full day of stand-up ? Name names . Call them out . Throw down the gauntlet . &lt;/strong&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; There&amp;rsquo ; s so many talented comedians in this city , I&amp;rsquo ; m sure we could take on a whole month ! &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; *** &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;em&gt;Graham Clark 's 24 Hours of Standup For Charity begins Friday , Nov. 29 in Vancouver 's tiny Havana Theatre at 1212 Commercial Drive . Tickets are available at the door and &lt;a href= " http : **29;66101;TOOLONG " &gt; ... here&lt;/a&gt;. &lt;/em&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;a href= " **28;66132;TOOLONG " **35;66162;TOOLONG &lt;/p&gt; &lt;a href= " https : **28;66199;TOOLONG " class= " twitter-follow-button " data-show-count= " false " &gt;Follow **25;66229;TOOLONG &lt;hr&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;em&gt;&lt;a href= " **27;66256;TOOLONG " &gt;CLICK HERE&lt;/a&gt; to report a typo. &lt;/em&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;em&gt;Is there more to this story ? " data-portal-copyright= " Malcolm Parry " **28;66470;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1144515 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; PENDER&amp;rsquo ; S BIG SPENDERS : Time was when future notables vacated downtown theatres and bars to dine on neon-glittering , late-night Pender Street . That migration reversed recently when Lieutenant-Governor Janet Austin , Premier John Horgan , Mayor Kennedy Stewart and other heavies attended the Vancouver Chinatown Foundation&amp;rsquo ; s third annual fundraising gala in the Fairmont Hotel Vancouver . No old-time chow mein , foo yung or sweet-and-sour pork was served . Neither were barbecued duck , soy chicken or curry beef brisket from foundation chair Carol Lee&amp;rsquo ; s two-year-old Chinatown BBQ . Instead , gala co-chairs Sam Feldman , Darlene Poole and attendees ate ahi tuna carpaccio and mushroom-crusted beef filet while generating a reported &amp;mdash ; and remarkable &amp;mdash ; $5.5 million . That will help build the foundation&amp;rsquo ; s proposed 230-home 58 West Hastings project with integrated health-care facilities . Lee&amp;rsquo ; s Chinatown history includes entrepreneur-grandfather Ron Bick Lee arriving from Guangdong in 1911 and living to be 104 . Accompanied by siblings and mother Lily , Lee doubtless missed 86-year-old father Robert , the Prospero International Realty founder , who is ailing . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;img src= " https : **34;66500;TOOLONG ... title= " Mandy Carnahan and hundreds of others dressed accordingly when the Little Black Dress charity event filled a Fairmont Vancouver Hotel ballroom . " data-portal-copyright= " Malcolm Parry " **28;66934;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1144512 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; SHINING LIGHT : One of Nazi Germany&amp;rsquo ; s darkly predictive incidents was recalled at the Jewish Cultural Centre&amp;rsquo ; s Holocaust Education Centre recently . Internist-human geneticist Michael Hayden spoke and presented an exhibition based on the lives of grandparents Max and Gertrud Hahn whose artworks and other property were stolen on Nov. 10 , 1938 . That was Kristallnacht &amp;mdash ; Night of Broken Glass &amp;mdash ; when Jewish homes , businesses , schools and hospitals were looted , 267 synagogues destroyed , 30,000 Jews sent to concentration camps ( the Hahns died in one ) and unknown others perished . Recovered from the Hahns&amp;rsquo ; looted collection , an 18th-century silver-gilt kiddush cup is in the exhibition which runs to Nov. 27 . Occurring on the eve of Remembrance Day , the Nazi-regime-encouraged Kristallnacht remains a horrific reminder of how civilization&amp;rsquo ; s veneer can be stripped away . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;img src= " https : **34;66964;TOOLONG ... title= " Ocean Wise executive chef Ned Bell recruited others to prepare a gala banquet with each course reminding guests of plastic pollution in global waters . It &amp;ldquo ; drew attention to plastic pollution and the opportunity for innovative solutions . &amp;rdquo ; The newly installed association president-CEO , Lasse Gustavsson , saw executive chef Ned Bell and volunteer colleagues serve banquet courses that each addressed the event&amp;rsquo ; s theme . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; SETTING IT STRAIGHT : Lawyer Seema Lal and hotelier Philip Meyer&amp;rsquo ; s names were misspelled Nov. 9 &lt;/p&gt; &lt;img src= " https : **34;67030;TOOLONG ... title= " Jack Webster was pictured in a 1970s broadcast studio equipped with two microphones , a reel-to-reel tape deck , rotary-dial phone and brimming ashtray . " alt= " Jack Webster was pictured in a 1970s broadcast studio equipped with two microphones , a reel-to-reel tape deck , rotary-dial phone and brimming ashtray . " data-portal-copyright= " Malcolm Parry " **28;67066;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1144508 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; JACK BE QUICK : The recent Jack Webster Awards named Vancouver Sun journalist Daphne Bramham Commentator of The Year . Retired Postmedia colleague Fabian Dawson received the Bruce Hutchison Lifetime Achievement Award . Both would remember print-radio-TV journalist Webster , who died in 1999 , although younger awardees and nominees might not . Accustomed to today&amp;rsquo ; s digital aids , the latter might marvel at the low-tech appurtenances of Webster&amp;rsquo ; s 1960s-1970s radio heyday . A contemporary photo shows a reel-to-reel tape machine , rotary-dial phone and butt-filled ashtray on his broadcast-studio desk . But native Scot Webster had one reporting aid forgotten today : shorthand . Learned at U.K. newspapers , that high-speed notetaking enabled fledgling Sun reporter Webster to outperform others during a sexually lurid 1950 capital-murder trial that made his reputation and &amp;ldquo ; $300 in overtime &amp;mdash ; unheard of in those days . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; DOWN PARRYSCOPE : Before sampling all the Beluga vodka cocktails created for this year&amp;rsquo ; s International Chess Federation tournament , maybe check with your mate . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;a href= " **27;67096;TOOLONG " **34;67125;TOOLONG &lt;br&gt; 604-929-8456 &lt;/p&gt; Malcolm ParryFri , 15 Nov 2019 19:04:23 **45;67161;TOOLONG ... &amp;amp ; ChildFashion &amp;amp ; **28;67208;TOOLONG TravelLocal ArtsLocal **25;67238;TOOLONG Jamalzadah : The Oprah of Afghanistan tells her story in Voice of Rebellionhttp : **31;67265;TOOLONG ... &lt;img src= " https : **34;67298;TOOLONG ... title= " Mozhdah Jamalzadah . Afghani-Canadian broadcaster and singer and subject of the book Voice of Rebellion . 2019 PNG Merlin Archive " alt= " Mozhdah Jamalzadah . Afghani-Canadian broadcaster and singer and subject of the book Voice of Rebellion . 2019 PNG Merlin Archive " data-portal-copyright= " " **28;67334;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1137228 " /&gt; &lt;h3&gt;Voice of Rebellion&lt;/h3&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;strong&gt;How Mozhdah Jamalzadah Brought Hope to **26;67364;TOOLONG &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; By Roberta Staley ( Greystone Books , Vancouver ) &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; $32.95 280 pp. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;hr&gt; &lt;p&gt; In this rags-to-righteousness story , author Roberta Staley charts how Mozhdah Jamalzadah went from being a child refugee arriving in Canada to hosting the barrier-breaking Mozhdah Show for two years and earning the moniker , &amp;ldquo ; the Oprah of Afghanistan&amp;rdquo ; in her 20s. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Tackling such taboo subjects as divorce and domestic violence earned the dynamic young broadcaster a fierce fan base matched only by an even-fiercer opposition . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; As the book documents , there came a time when it was no longer safe to continue working in Kabul . But by the time that occurred , her reputation was such that she sang at the White House , met her idol Oprah Winfrey and accomplished much more . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; With her career in her ancestral homeland cut short , Jamalzadah continues her activism from abroad , pursuing a musical and acting career from her Vancouver home . The cover image of her wearing the blue burqa turned up to reveal her face is loaded with defiance and contradictions . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; When her family &amp;mdash ; educated and modern &amp;mdash ; took a midnight bus from Afghanistan to Pakistan , her mother , Nasrin , wore the full burqa to pass as an obedient , passive peasant wife through the many checkpoints along the way . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Mozhdah discusses how a symbol of female subjugation had become a means to freedom impacted upon her later choices from everything to how she appeared on TV to how she lives her life today . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;img src= " https : **34;67392;TOOLONG ... title= " " alt= " " data-portal-copyright= " " **28;67428;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; Voice of Rebellion . Book about Mozhadeh Jamalzah by author Roberta Staley . Greystone. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; During the Civil War , the whole country changed and a whole Soviet-style surveillance culture developed that had everyone looking over their shoulder , &amp;rdquo ; said Jamalzadah. &amp;ldquo ; Then during the Taliban era , there was this element of fear added to it . Even afterwards , there would be moments when rumours or news would go around that would get everyone in a state of heightened tension again . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Coming to Canada from that unsettled war zone was an impulsive decision when her father was warned the police were coming for him . The family made it to Pakistan as refugees , where life was basically hand to mouth . Permitted to immigrate to Canada , the family landed in Oshawa , Ont. , where everything from snow piled high to rampant classroom racism took its toll on the impressionable young Mozhdah. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Moving to Vancouver proved a much different experience in the more multicultural classroom and population mix . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The passages about bullying and the hardships of the immigrant experience in Voice of Rebellion prove to be a potent antidote to the bigoted masses making up their own headlines in social media that the mainstream press often proliferates. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; How the family rebuilt itself through diligent work , smart business decisions and dedication out of nothing is inspirational enough . But where Mozhdah takes it is exceptional . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Setting her father&amp;rsquo ; s protest poem , titled &lt;em&gt;Dukhtare Afghan&lt;/em&gt; , about acid attacks on girls walking to school , to music , Mohzdah became a chart-topping singer and was invited to perform the song in front of the Obamas at the White House . The status she attained from the song and other musical appearances lead to the offer to host a show on the new 1TV station in Kabul . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Heading back to her ancestral homeland , she found herself directionless again . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;iframe width= " 640 " height= " 480 " src= " https : **34;67458;TOOLONG ... frameborder= " 0 " allow= " accelerometer ; autoplay ; encrypted-media ; gyroscope ; picture-in-picture " **34;67494;TOOLONG &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; It&amp;rsquo ; s the story of my life . This complete cultural shock of being somewhere completely different all over again , trying to define my space there all over again and again , &amp;rdquo ; she said . &amp;ldquo ; Here , I&amp;rsquo ; m seen as ethnic , as an immigrant , and English as a second language-speaker. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; In Afghanistan , I&amp;rsquo ; m this Westerner in their eyes trying to speak Farsi for an entire one-hour show . That was one of the hardest things I&amp;rsquo ; ve ever had to do . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; But she did it , and the show was a game-changer in Afghanistan . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Inspired by the Oprah Winfrey Show , the Mozhdah Show was unlike anything on the air . Topics ranged from domestic violence to the lives of spandis &amp;mdash ; young people who begged on the street waving burning cans of herbs at motorists to ward off evil . The programs hit a nerve in a war-torn country emerging from a tumultuous few decades . Not everyone loved it , but she kept at it . Then an assault by a co-worker and mounting serious death threats made it impossible to continue . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; All of that changes you as a person , and the strength you gain coming out on the other end of it doesn&amp;rsquo ; t come without a cost , &amp;rdquo ; she said . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; I went there really optimistic , naively thinking that I could help and make things possible and , perhaps because of that , it actually did for a time . When it didn&amp;rsquo ; t , it set me back and I almost fell into a very dark hole where I couldn&amp;rsquo ; t see the light , or get myself back up and be where I was before . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; She stresses the experience gained from her time in Kabul reinforces her convictions that there are good people everywhere being taken advantage of by people in power manipulating situations to their benefits . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; If anything , the experience has opened her eyes to the fact that issues such as the subjugation of women are global in nature , that bad apples fall from all trees regardless of their cultural or religious practices and the main thing is to keep standing up to the negative and work toward the positive . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; Of all the things that me and my 19-year-old producer brought in as subjects for the show , nothing was as rewarding as introducing the concept of positive thinking , &amp;rdquo ; she said . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; Decades of war has instilled a very negative mindset in everyone , and everything is negative and in denial . So for a couple of episodes , I talked about how positive thinking could change your day , you life , your way of thinking and even that met with serious opposition . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; But she says people still comment how important raising the topic was . She thinks there is a platform for spreading the word to future generations of young people &amp;ldquo ; messed up , depressed and bullied by social media , &amp;rdquo ; as well as hearing the immigrant voice around all manner of issues in the news . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Jamalzadah hopes that &lt;em&gt;Voice of Rebellion&lt;/em&gt; will inspire other young women to pursue the difficult , but rewarding , path of breaking down barriers and developing dialogue around difficult issues . The way her story reads , it&amp;rsquo ; s not difficult to imagine a scriptwriter phoning to option it for a new series on one of the many streaming services out there . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Given that she posts regular videos on her label &lt;a href= " https : **32;67530;TOOLONG ... target= " blank " rel= " noopener " &gt;Ethnobeast&lt;/a&gt; &amp;lsquo ; s YouTube channel , it&amp;rsquo ; s obvious we haven&amp;rsquo ; t heard the last from this rebellious voice . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;iframe width= " 640 " height= " 360 " src= " https : **34;67564;TOOLONG ... frameborder= " 0 " allow= " accelerometer ; autoplay ; encrypted-media ; gyroscope ; picture-in-picture " **34;67600;TOOLONG &lt;p&gt; &lt;a href= " **29;67636;TOOLONG " target= " blank " rel= " noopener " **36;67667;TOOLONG &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;a href= " https : **27;67705;TOOLONG " target= " blank " rel= " noopener " **29;67734;TOOLONG &l; ... &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;em&gt;&lt;span class= " s1 " &gt;&lt;a href= " **27;67765;TOOLONG " &gt;CLICK **35;67794;TOOLONG class= " s2 " &gt; to report a typo. &lt;/span&gt;&lt;/em&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;em&gt;&lt;span class= " s2 " &gt;Is there more to this story ? Handler brings Life Will Be the Death Of Me standup tour to Canadahttp : **34;67967;TOOLONG ... &lt;img src= " https : **34;68003;TOOLONG ... title= " Chelsea Handler . " alt= " Chelsea Handler . " data-portal-copyright= " Handout " **28;68039;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1132365 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;strong&gt;Life Will Be the Death of Me : &lt;a href= " http : //chelseahandler.com " target= " blank " rel= " noopener " &gt;Chelsea Handler&lt;/a&gt; 's Stand-Up Comedy Tour&lt;/strong&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;strong&gt;When : &lt;/strong&gt; Nov. 7-8 , 8 p.m. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;strong&gt;Where : &lt;/strong&gt; Chan Shun Concert Hall , 6265 Crescent Rd. , UBC &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;strong&gt;Tickets and info : &lt;/strong&gt; &lt;a href= " https : **33;68069;TOOLONG ... target= " blank " rel= " noopener " **28;68104;TOOLONG &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; There aren&amp;rsquo ; t many authors who can lay claim to having five of their six books debut at No. 1 on the New York Times Bestseller List . Chelsea Handler can . Her latest read , titled Life Will Be the Death of Me , explores a year of self-discovery undertaken by the author to exorcise some personal demons such as the death of her brother , and come out on the other end all the better . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; It&amp;rsquo ; s a change of direction for the writer best known for blunt dissections of sex , drinking and other assorted activities in books such as Are You There , Vodka ? It&amp;rsquo ; s Me , Chelsea ( 2008 ) , or Lies that Chelsea Handler Told Me ( 2011 ) , and Netflix series such as Chelsea Does or last month&amp;rsquo ; s Hello , Privilege , It&amp;rsquo ; s Me , Chelsea . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The book also heralds Handler&amp;rsquo ; s return to standup &amp;mdash ; actually , sit-down &amp;mdash ; comedy as she embarks on a massive tour in support of the text . Digging deeply into the pain of her childhood loss put the comedian in a very different frame of mind . She remains the self-deprecating New Jersey-ite fans adore , but there is a renewed passion for activism around personal and political issues , as well as everyone&amp;rsquo ; s favourite herb . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; We begin our discussion around the line of marijuana products that Handler is launching in 2020 , and how one launches a line of cannabis products . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; You have to do a lot of research , into tinctures , oils , lotions , bath salts , weed and edibles , and I made it my priority for the past 2 1/2 years to do that , &amp;rdquo ; said Handler . &amp;ldquo ; It was a surprising pivot from alcohol and one I far prefer , but it makes international travel challenging . I&amp;rsquo ; m back on the sauce here in the U.K. , so I&amp;rsquo ; m looking forward to coming to Canada . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Advised of the continuing supply shortages , slow pace of introducing full product lines into the marketplace and the vibrant black market , she zeros in on the irony that the legal marijuana business is being run by the same people that used to jail people for minor offences and they&amp;rsquo ; re doing it poorly . Handler says that she and her partners at NorCal Cannabis are looking at organizations representing people who are in jail for minor possession charges . The plan is to donate a portion of the proceeds to getting people out . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; She also wants women to expand the cannabis category to include products specifically developed for women , who she feels have been ignored for the most part . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; I&amp;rsquo ; ve discovered with micro-dosing that it&amp;rsquo ; s a whole different ball game and I want women and first-time users to know that it&amp;rsquo ; s a safe place , where you can get high and be emboldened and empowered instead of having negative experiences , &amp;rdquo ; she said . &amp;ldquo ; I want to look into developing the THCV strain , which is an appetite suppressant , and has been completely ignored by the guys who have been in charge of marketing weed for the past 100 years . Often , the biggest gripe from women around using weed is , &amp;lsquo ; I&amp;rsquo ; ll eat my face off , &amp;rsquo ; and that&amp;rsquo ; s not true . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; There certainly do seem to be snack-food-filled emporiums located within a few paces of many of our local cannabis retail outlets . A way to combat &amp;ldquo ; convenience&amp;rdquo ; food cravings might provide health benefits to all kinds of users . Handler says to wait and see in the new year , which is when the product line should be launching . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Until then , she has plenty on the go . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Hello , Privilege , It&amp;rsquo ; s Me , Chelsea , found her trying to dive into the issue of what white privilege is and how blind , or antagonistic to it , white people are to the subject . Beginning with Chandler herself , who has admitted in interviews that she likes to &amp;ldquo ; get her opinion changed . &amp;rdquo ; The show has drawn mixed reviews , with many media outlets noting Chandler&amp;rsquo ; s controversial past jokes and actions as problematic . The comic doesn&amp;rsquo ; t deny it , but says that her humour is sharper now that she is more educated around the issues around racism , sexism and so on . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The election of Donald Trump to the office of U.S. president proved to be a major catalyst for her creative energy , and bringing the contents of her new book to the stage on this tour has been rewarding . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; I wasn&amp;rsquo ; t planning on doing the book , or on doing standup , but then the book just vomited out of me and I had something that I could take on the road as a standup show , &amp;rdquo ; she said . &amp;ldquo ; I&amp;rsquo ; ve taken the subject matter from the book , made it side-splitting and that&amp;rsquo ; s always been something that I really wanted . To be a standup with a message that wasn&amp;rsquo ; t just silly jokes , but had a story to go with it . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; There will be another Netflix special coming , filmed on the closing days of this tour . Life Will Be the Death of Me has been optioned for a TV series and Chandler plans on starring in it . She&amp;rsquo ; s not sure that anyone but her could play her at this stage in her life . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; This tour , and this book has been more personal than anything else I&amp;rsquo ; ve ever done before and that authenticity shows , &amp;rdquo ; she said . &amp;ldquo ; People respond to that in different ways , and it&amp;rsquo ; s nicer to be out there not being a loud-mouthed b&amp;mdash ; h and spreading something more positive instead . After the ( U.S. ) election , it became so painfully obvious how rampant racism and sexism were , and still are , and I wanted to be part of the solution instead of part of the problem . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; She says that her business is an easy one to live with your head buried so far up an orifice that you don&amp;rsquo ; t see or notice anything around you . But once you open your eyes , there is an awful lot to see .
